99:Tfd1,<p>The descriptions below in this section, <emu-xref href="#sec-atomics-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-atomics-object">25.4</a></emu-xref>, and <emu-xref href="#sec-memory-model"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model">29</a></emu-xref> use the read-modify-write modification function internal data structure.</p>
      <p>A <dfn variants="read-modify-write modification functions">read-modify-write modification function</dfn> is a mathematical function that is represented as an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure">Abstract Closure</a></emu-xref> that takes two <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">Lists</a></emu-xref> of <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-data-blocks"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-data-blocks">byte values</a></emu-xref> as arguments and returns a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> of <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-data-blocks"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-data-blocks">byte values</a></emu-xref>. These <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure">Abstract Closures</a></emu-xref> satisfy all of the following properties:</p>
      <ul>
        <li>They perform all their algorithm steps atomically.</li>
        <li>Their individual algorithm steps are not observable.</li>
      </ul>
      <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>To aid verifying that a read-modify-write modification function's algorithm steps constitute a pure, mathematical function, the following editorial conventions are recommended:</p>
        <ul>
          <li>They do not access, directly or transitively via invoked <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations">abstract operations</a></emu-xref> and <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure">Abstract Closures</a></emu-xref>, any language or specification values except their parameters and captured values.</li>
          <li>They do not invoke, directly or transitively, <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations">abstract operations</a></emu-xref> and <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure">Abstract Closures</a></emu-xref> that return <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">Completion Records</a></emu-xref>.</li>
          <li>They do not return <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">Completion Records</a></emu-xref>.</li>
        </ul>
      </div></emu-note>

bc:T6e0,<p><code>ArrayBuffer[%Symbol.species%]</code> is an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type">accessor property</a></emu-xref> whose set accessor function is <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>.         <emu-alg><ol><li class="exit">Return the <emu-val>this</emu-val> value.</li></ol></emu-alg>
        <p>The value of the <emu-val>"name"</emu-val> property of this function is <emu-val>"get [Symbol.species]"</emu-val>.</p>

c8:T580,<p>ArrayBuffer instances inherit properties from the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-properties-of-the-arraybuffer-prototype-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-properties-of-the-arraybuffer-prototype-object">ArrayBuffer prototype object</a></emu-xref>. ArrayBuffer instances each have an <var class="field">[[ArrayBufferData]]</var> internal slot, an <var class="field">[[ArrayBufferByteLength]]</var> internal slot, and an <var class="field">[[ArrayBufferDetachKey]]</var> internal slot. ArrayBuffer instances which are resizable each have an <var class="field">[[ArrayBufferMaxByteLength]]</var> internal slot.</p>
      <p>ArrayBuffer instances whose <var class="field">[[ArrayBufferData]]</var> is <emu-val>null</emu-val> are considered to be detached and all operators to access or modify data contained in the ArrayBuffer instance will fail.</p>
      <p>ArrayBuffer instances whose <var class="field">[[ArrayBufferDetachKey]]</var> is set to a value other than <emu-val>undefined</emu-val> need to have all <emu-xref aoid="DetachArrayBuffer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-detacharraybuffer">DetachArrayBuffer</a></emu-xref> calls passing that same “detach key” as an argument, otherwise a TypeError will result. This internal slot is only ever set by certain embedding environments, not by algorithms in this specification.</p>

c9:T1045,<emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>The following are guidelines for ECMAScript programmers working with <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-resizable-arraybuffer-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-resizable-arraybuffer-objects">resizable ArrayBuffer</a></emu-xref>.</p>
        <p>We recommend that programs be tested in their deployment environments where possible. The amount of available physical memory differs greatly between hardware devices. Similarly, virtual memory subsystems also differ greatly between hardware devices as well as operating systems. An application that runs without out-of-memory errors on a 64-bit desktop web browser could run out of memory on a 32-bit mobile web browser.</p>
        <p>When choosing a value for the <emu-val>"maxByteLength"</emu-val> option for <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-resizable-arraybuffer-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-resizable-arraybuffer-objects">resizable ArrayBuffer</a></emu-xref>, we recommend that the smallest possible size for the application be chosen. We recommend that <emu-val>"maxByteLength"</emu-val> does not exceed 1,073,741,824 (2<sup>30</sup> bytes or 1GiB).</p>
        <p>Please note that successfully constructing a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-resizable-arraybuffer-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-resizable-arraybuffer-objects">resizable ArrayBuffer</a></emu-xref> for a particular maximum size does not guarantee that future resizes will succeed.</p>
      </div></emu-note>

      <emu-note data-num="2"><span class="note">Note 2</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>The following are guidelines for ECMAScript implementers implementing <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-resizable-arraybuffer-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-resizable-arraybuffer-objects">resizable ArrayBuffer</a></emu-xref>.</p>
        <p><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-resizable-arraybuffer-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-resizable-arraybuffer-objects">Resizable ArrayBuffer</a></emu-xref> can be implemented as copying upon resize, as in-place growth via reserving virtual memory up front, or as a combination of both for different values of the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor">constructor</a></emu-xref>'s <emu-val>"maxByteLength"</emu-val> option.</p>
        <p>If a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host">host</a></emu-xref> is multi-tenanted (i.e. it runs many ECMAScript applications simultaneously), such as a web browser, and its implementations choose to implement in-place growth by reserving virtual memory, we recommend that both 32-bit and 64-bit implementations throw for values of <emu-val>"maxByteLength"</emu-val> ≥ 1GiB to 1.5GiB. This is to reduce the likelihood a single application can exhaust the virtual memory address space and to reduce interoperability risk.</p>
        <p>If a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host">host</a></emu-xref> does not have virtual memory, such as those running on embedded devices without an MMU, or if a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host">host</a></emu-xref> only implements resizing by copying, it may accept any <emu-not-ref>Number value for</emu-not-ref> the <emu-val>"maxByteLength"</emu-val> option. However, we recommend a <emu-val>RangeError</emu-val> be thrown if a memory block of the requested size can never be allocated. For example, if the requested size is greater than the maximum amount of usable memory on the device.</p>
      </div></emu-note>

d3:Tb0f,<p>A <dfn variants="DataView With Buffer Witness Records">DataView With Buffer Witness Record</dfn> is a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">Record</a></emu-xref> value used to encapsulate a DataView along with a cached byte length of the viewed buffer. It is used to help ensure there is a single <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model-fundamentals"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model-fundamentals">ReadSharedMemory</a></emu-xref> event of the byte length data block when the viewed buffer is a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-growable-sharedarraybuffer-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-growable-sharedarraybuffer-objects">growable SharedArrayBuffer</a></emu-xref>.</p>
        <p>DataView With Buffer Witness Records have the fields listed in <emu-xref href="#table-dataview-with-buffer-witness-record-fields"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#table-dataview-with-buffer-witness-record-fields">Table 69</a></emu-xref>.</p>
        <emu-table id="table-dataview-with-buffer-witness-record-fields" caption="DataView With Buffer Witness Record Fields" data-num="69">

dd:T9e2,<p>The Atomics object:</p>
    <ul>
      <li>is <dfn><emu-intrinsic>%Atomics%</emu-intrinsic></dfn>.</li>
      <li>is the initial value of the <emu-val>"Atomics"</emu-val> property of the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/global-object#sec-global-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/global-object#sec-global-object">global object</a></emu-xref>.</li>
      <li>is an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#ordinary-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#ordinary-object">ordinary object</a></emu-xref>.</li>
      <li>has a <var class="field">[[Prototype]]</var> internal slot whose value is <emu-intrinsic>%Object.prototype%</emu-intrinsic>.</li>
      <li>does not have a <var class="field">[[Construct]]</var> internal method; it cannot be used as a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor">constructor</a></emu-xref> with the <code>new</code> operator.</li>
      <li>does not have a <var class="field">[[Call]]</var> internal method; it cannot be invoked as a function.</li>
    </ul>
    <p>The Atomics object provides functions that operate indivisibly (atomically) on shared memory array cells as well as functions that let <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent">agents</a></emu-xref> wait for and dispatch primitive events. When used with discipline, the Atomics functions allow multi-<emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent">agent</a></emu-xref> programs that communicate through shared memory to execute in a well-understood order even on parallel CPUs. The rules that govern shared-memory communication are provided by the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model">memory model</a></emu-xref>, defined below.</p>
    <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
      <p>For informative guidelines for programming and implementing shared memory in ECMAScript, please see the notes at the end of the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model">memory model</a></emu-xref> section.</p>
    </div></emu-note>

f0:T694,<p>The JSON object:</p>
    <ul>
      <li>is <dfn><emu-intrinsic>%JSON%</emu-intrinsic></dfn>.</li>
      <li>is the initial value of the <emu-val>"JSON"</emu-val> property of the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/global-object#sec-global-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/global-object#sec-global-object">global object</a></emu-xref>.</li>
      <li>is an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#ordinary-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#ordinary-object">ordinary object</a></emu-xref>.</li>
      <li>contains two functions, <code>parse</code> and <code>stringify</code>, that are used to parse and construct JSON texts.</li>
      <li>has a <var class="field">[[Prototype]]</var> internal slot whose value is <emu-intrinsic>%Object.prototype%</emu-intrinsic>.</li>
      <li>does not have a <var class="field">[[Construct]]</var> internal method; it cannot be used as a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor">constructor</a></emu-xref> with the <code>new</code> operator.</li>
      <li>does not have a <var class="field">[[Call]]</var> internal method; it cannot be invoked as a function.</li>
    </ul>
    <p>The JSON Data Interchange Format is defined in ECMA-404. The JSON interchange format used in this specification is exactly that described by ECMA-404. Conforming implementations of <code>JSON.parse</code> and <code>JSON.stringify</code> must support the exact interchange format described in the ECMA-404 specification without any deletions or extensions to the format.</p>

    f1:Te2c,<p>This function parses a JSON text (a JSON-formatted String) and produces an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types">ECMAScript language value</a></emu-xref>. The JSON format represents literals, arrays, and objects with a syntax similar to the syntax for ECMAScript literals, Array Initializers, and Object Initializers. After parsing, JSON objects are realized as ECMAScript objects. JSON arrays are realized as ECMAScript Array instances. JSON strings, numbers, booleans, and null are realized as ECMAScript Strings, Numbers, Booleans, and <emu-val>null</emu-val>.</p>
      <p>The optional <var>reviver</var> parameter is a function that can filter and transform the results. For each value produced by the parse, <var>reviver</var> is called with three arguments (the associated <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-key"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-key">property key</a></emu-xref>, the value, and a context object). If the property is unmodified and its value is primitive, the provided context object has a <emu-val>"source"</emu-val> property containing source text of the corresponding <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-syntactic-grammar"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-syntactic-grammar">Parse Node</a></emu-xref>. If the call returns <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>, the property is deleted. Otherwise, the property is redefined to use the return value.</p>
      <emu-alg><ol><li>Let <var>jsonString</var> be ? <emu-xref aoid="ToString"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-tostring">ToString</a></emu-xref>(<var>text</var>).</li><li>Let <var>parseResult</var> be ? <emu-xref aoid="ParseJSON"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-ParseJSON">ParseJSON</a></emu-xref>(<var>jsonString</var>).</li><li>Let <var>unfiltered</var> be <var>parseResult</var>.<var class="field">[[Value]]</var>.</li><li class="exit">If <emu-xref aoid="IsCallable"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-iscallable">IsCallable</a></emu-xref>(<var>reviver</var>) is <emu-val>false</emu-val>, return <var>unfiltered</var>.</li><li>Let <var>root</var> be <emu-xref aoid="OrdinaryObjectCreate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#sec-ordinaryobjectcreate">OrdinaryObjectCreate</a></emu-xref>(<emu-intrinsic>%Object.prototype%</emu-intrinsic>).</li><li>Let <var>rootName</var> be the empty String.</li><li>Perform ! <emu-xref aoid="CreateDataPropertyOrThrow"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-createdatapropertyorthrow">CreateDataPropertyOrThrow</a></emu-xref>(<var>root</var>, <var>rootName</var>, <var>unfiltered</var>).</li><li>Let <var>snapshot</var> be <emu-meta suppress-effects="user-code"><emu-xref aoid="CreateJSONParseRecord"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-createjsonparserecord">CreateJSONParseRecord</a></emu-xref>(<var>parseResult</var>.<var class="field">[[ParseNode]]</var>, <var>rootName</var>, <var>unfiltered</var>)</emu-meta>.</li><li class="exit">Return ? <emu-xref aoid="InternalizeJSONProperty"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-internalizejsonproperty">InternalizeJSONProperty</a></emu-xref>(<var>root</var>, <var>rootName</var>, <var>reviver</var>, <var>snapshot</var>).</li></ol></emu-alg>

        <emu-alg><ol><li>If <var>type</var> is <emu-const>float16</emu-const>, then<ol><li>Let <var>rawBytes</var> be a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> whose elements are the 2 bytes that are the result of converting <var>value</var> to <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/bibliography#sec-bibliography"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/bibliography#sec-bibliography">IEEE 754-2019</a></emu-xref> binary16 format using roundTiesToEven mode. The bytes are arranged in little endian order. It gives the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host">host</a></emu-xref> an opportunity to perform <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined">implementation-defined</a></emu-xref> growing of <var>buffer</var>. If the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host">host</a></emu-xref> chooses not to handle growing of <var>buffer</var>, it may return <emu-const>unhandled</emu-const> for the default behaviour.</p>
        <p>The implementation of HostGrowSharedArrayBuffer must conform to the following requirements:</p>
        <ul>
          <li>If the abstract operation does not complete normally with <emu-const>unhandled</emu-const>, and <var>newByteLength</var> &lt; the current byte length of the <var>buffer</var> or <var>newByteLength</var> > <var>buffer</var>.<var class="field">[[ArrayBufferMaxByteLength]]</var>, throw a <emu-val>RangeError</emu-val> exception.</li>
          <li>Let <var>agentRecord</var> be the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent-record"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent-record">Agent Record</a></emu-xref> of the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#surrounding-agent"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#surrounding-agent">surrounding agent</a></emu-xref>. Let <var>isLittleEndian</var> be <var>agentRecord</var>.<var class="field">[[LittleEndian]]</var>. If the abstract operation completes normally with <emu-const>handled</emu-const>, a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model-fundamentals"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model-fundamentals">WriteSharedMemory</a></emu-xref> or <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model-fundamentals"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model-fundamentals">ReadModifyWriteSharedMemory</a></emu-xref> event whose <var class="field">[[Order]]</var> is <emu-const>seq-cst</emu-const>, <var class="field">[[Payload]]</var> is <emu-xref aoid="NumericToRawBytes"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-numerictorawbytes">NumericToRawBytes</a></emu-xref>(<emu-const>biguint64</emu-const>, <var>newByteLength</var>, <var>isLittleEndian</var>), <var class="field">[[Block]]</var> is <var>buffer</var>.<var class="field">[[ArrayBufferByteLengthData]]</var>, <var class="field">[[ByteIndex]]</var> is 0, and <var class="field">[[ElementSize]]</var> is 8 is added to the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#surrounding-agent"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#surrounding-agent">surrounding agent</a></emu-xref>'s <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-candidate-executions"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-candidate-executions">candidate execution</a></emu-xref> such that racing calls to <emu-xref href="#sec-sharedarraybuffer.prototype.grow" title><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-sharedarraybuffer.prototype.grow">SharedArrayBuffer.prototype.grow ( <var>newLength</var> )</a></emu-xref> are not “lost”, i.e. silently do nothing.</li>
        </ul>

        <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>The second requirement above is intentionally vague about how or when the current byte length of <var>buffer</var> is read. Because the byte length must be updated via an atomic read-modify-write operation on the underlying hardware, architectures that use load-link/store-conditional or load-exclusive/store-exclusive instruction pairs may wish to keep the paired instructions close in the instruction stream. As such, <emu-xref href="#sec-sharedarraybuffer.prototype.grow" title><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-sharedarraybuffer.prototype.grow">SharedArrayBuffer.prototype.grow ( <var>newLength</var> )</a></emu-xref> itself does not perform bounds checking on <var>newByteLength</var> before calling HostGrowSharedArrayBuffer, nor is there a requirement on when the current byte length is read.</p>
          <p>This is in contrast with <emu-xref aoid="HostResizeArrayBuffer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-hostresizearraybuffer">HostResizeArrayBuffer</a></emu-xref>, which is guaranteed that 0 ≤ <var>newByteLength</var> ≤ <var>buffer</var>.<var class="field">[[ArrayBufferMaxByteLength]]</var>.</p>
        </div></emu-note>

        <p>The default implementation of HostGrowSharedArrayBuffer is to return <emu-xref aoid="NormalCompletion"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-normalcompletion">NormalCompletion</a></emu-xref>(<emu-const>unhandled</emu-const>).</p>
      cd:["$","emu-clause",null,{"id":"sec-hostgrowsharedarraybuffer","children":[["$","h1",null,{"id":"25224-hostgrowsharedarraybuffer--buffer-newbytelength-","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-bold x:mt-2 x:text-4xl","children":[[["$","span",null,{"className":"secnum","children":"25.2.2.4"}]," HostGrowSharedArrayBuffer ( ",["$","var",null,{"children":"buffer"}],", ",["$","var",null,{"children":"newByteLength"}]," )"],["$","$L98",null,{"id":"25224-hostgrowsharedarraybuffer--buffer-newbytelength-"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"$10f"}}]]}]
110:T9ce,<p>The SharedArrayBuffer <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor">constructor</a></emu-xref>:</p>
      <ul>
        <li>is <dfn><emu-intrinsic>%SharedArrayBuffer%</emu-intrinsic></dfn>.</li>
        <li>is the initial value of the <emu-val>"SharedArrayBuffer"</emu-val> property of the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/global-object#sec-global-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/global-object#sec-global-object">global object</a></emu-xref>, if that property is present (see below).</li>
        <li>creates and initializes a new SharedArrayBuffer when called as a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor">constructor</a></emu-xref>.</li>
        <li>is not intended to be called as a function and will throw an exception when called in that manner.</li>
        <li>may be used as the value of an <code>extends</code> clause of a class definition. Subclass <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor">constructors</a></emu-xref> that intend to inherit the specified SharedArrayBuffer behaviour must include a <code>super</code> call to the SharedArrayBuffer <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor">constructor</a></emu-xref> to create and initialize subclass instances with the internal state necessary to support the built-in methods of <code>SharedArrayBuffer.prototype</code>.</li>
      </ul>

      <p>Whenever a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host">host</a></emu-xref> does not provide concurrent access to SharedArrayBuffers it may omit the <emu-val>"SharedArrayBuffer"</emu-val> property of the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/global-object#sec-global-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/global-object#sec-global-object">global object</a></emu-xref>.</p>

      <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>Unlike an <code>ArrayBuffer</code>, a <code>SharedArrayBuffer</code> cannot become detached, and its internal <var class="field">[[ArrayBufferData]]</var> slot is never <emu-val>null</emu-val>.</p>
      </div></emu-note>

11a:T1e22,<emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>The following are guidelines for ECMAScript programmers working with <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-growable-sharedarraybuffer-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-growable-sharedarraybuffer-objects">growable SharedArrayBuffer</a></emu-xref>.</p>
        <p>We recommend that programs be tested in their deployment environments where possible. The amount of available physical memory differ greatly between hardware devices. Similarly, virtual memory subsystems also differ greatly between hardware devices as well as operating systems. An application that runs without out-of-memory errors on a 64-bit desktop web browser could run out of memory on a 32-bit mobile web browser.</p>
        <p>When choosing a value for the <emu-val>"maxByteLength"</emu-val> option for <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-growable-sharedarraybuffer-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-growable-sharedarraybuffer-objects">growable SharedArrayBuffer</a></emu-xref>, we recommend that the smallest possible size for the application be chosen. We recommend that <emu-val>"maxByteLength"</emu-val> does not exceed 1073741824, or 1GiB.</p>
        <p>Please note that successfully constructing a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-growable-sharedarraybuffer-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-growable-sharedarraybuffer-objects">growable SharedArrayBuffer</a></emu-xref> for a particular maximum size does not guarantee that future grows will succeed.</p>
        <p>Not all loads of a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-growable-sharedarraybuffer-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-growable-sharedarraybuffer-objects">growable SharedArrayBuffer</a></emu-xref>'s length are synchronizing <emu-const>seq-cst</emu-const> loads. Loads of the length that are for bounds-checking of an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#integer-index"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#integer-index">integer-indexed</a></emu-xref> property access, e.g. <code>u8[idx]</code>, are not synchronizing. In general, in the absence of explicit synchronization, one property access being in-bound does not imply a subsequent property access in the same <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent">agent</a></emu-xref> is also in-bound. In contrast, explicit loads of the length via the <code>length</code> and <code>byteLength</code> getters on SharedArrayBuffer, <emu-intrinsic>%TypedArray%</emu-intrinsic>.prototype, and DataView.prototype are synchronizing. Loads of the length that are performed by built-in methods to check if a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#typedarray"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#typedarray">TypedArray</a></emu-xref> is entirely out-of-bounds are also synchronizing.</p>
      </div></emu-note>

      <emu-note data-num="2"><span class="note">Note 2</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>The following are guidelines for ECMAScript implementers implementing <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-growable-sharedarraybuffer-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-growable-sharedarraybuffer-objects">growable SharedArrayBuffer</a></emu-xref>.</p>
        <p>We recommend <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-growable-sharedarraybuffer-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-growable-sharedarraybuffer-objects">growable SharedArrayBuffer</a></emu-xref> be implemented as in-place growth via reserving virtual memory up front.</p>
        <p>Because grow operations can happen in parallel with memory accesses on a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-growable-sharedarraybuffer-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-growable-sharedarraybuffer-objects">growable SharedArrayBuffer</a></emu-xref>, the constraints of the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model">memory model</a></emu-xref> require that even unordered accesses do not “tear” (bits of their values will not be mixed). In practice, this means the underlying data block of a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-growable-sharedarraybuffer-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-growable-sharedarraybuffer-objects">growable SharedArrayBuffer</a></emu-xref> cannot be grown by being copied without stopping the world. We do not recommend stopping the world as an implementation strategy because it introduces a serialization point and is slow.</p>
        <p>Grown memory must appear zeroed from the moment of its creation, including to any racy accesses in parallel. This can be accomplished via zero-filled-on-demand virtual memory pages, or careful synchronization if manually zeroing memory.</p>
        <p><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#integer-index"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#integer-index">Integer-indexed</a></emu-xref> property access on <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#typedarray"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#typedarray">TypedArray</a></emu-xref> views of growable SharedArrayBuffers is intended to be optimizable similarly to access on <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#typedarray"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#typedarray">TypedArray</a></emu-xref> views of non-growable SharedArrayBuffers, because <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#integer-index"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#integer-index">integer-indexed</a></emu-xref> property loads on are not synchronizing on the underlying buffer's length (see programmer guidelines above). For example, bounds checks for property accesses may still be hoisted out of loops.</p>
        <p>In practice it is difficult to implement <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-growable-sharedarraybuffer-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-fixed-length-and-growable-sharedarraybuffer-objects">growable SharedArrayBuffer</a></emu-xref> by copying on <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host">hosts</a></emu-xref> that do not have virtual memory, such as those running on embedded devices without an MMU. Memory usage behaviour of growable SharedArrayBuffers on such <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host">hosts</a></emu-xref> may significantly differ from that of <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host">hosts</a></emu-xref> with virtual memory. Such <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host">hosts</a></emu-xref> should clearly communicate memory usage expectations to users.</p>
      </div></emu-note>

13d:T4de,<p>DataView instances are <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#ordinary-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#ordinary-object">ordinary objects</a></emu-xref> that inherit properties from the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-properties-of-the-dataview-prototype-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-properties-of-the-dataview-prototype-object">DataView prototype object</a></emu-xref>. DataView instances each have <var class="field">[[DataView]]</var>, <var class="field">[[ViewedArrayBuffer]]</var>, <var class="field">[[ByteLength]]</var>, and <var class="field">[[ByteOffset]]</var> internal slots.</p>
      <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>The value of the <var class="field">[[DataView]]</var> internal slot is not used within this specification. The simple presence of that internal slot is used within the specification to identify objects created using the DataView <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor">constructor</a></emu-xref>.</p>

13e:T18b0,<p>A <dfn variants="WaiterList Records">WaiterList Record</dfn> is used to explain waiting and notification of <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent">agents</a></emu-xref> via <code>Atomics.wait</code>, <code>Atomics.waitAsync</code>, and <code>Atomics.notify</code>.</p>
      <p>A WaiterList Record has fields listed in <emu-xref href="#table-waiterlistrecord"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#table-waiterlistrecord">Table 71</a></emu-xref>.</p>
      <emu-table id="table-waiterlistrecord" caption="WaiterList Record Fields" data-num="71">
        <table>
          <thead>
            <tr>
              <th>
                Field Name
              </th>
              <th>
                Value
              </th>
              <th>
                Meaning
              </th>
            </tr>
          </thead>
          <tr>
            <td>
              <var class="field">[[Waiters]]</var>
            </td>
            <td>
              a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> of <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-waiter-record"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-waiter-record">Waiter Records</a></emu-xref>
            </td>
            <td>
              The calls to <code>Atomics.wait</code> or <code>Atomics.waitAsync</code> that are waiting on the location with which this WaiterList is associated.
            </td>
          </tr>
          <tr>
            <td>
              <var class="field">[[MostRecentLeaveEvent]]</var>
            </td>
            <td>
              a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model-fundamentals"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model-fundamentals">Synchronize event</a></emu-xref> or <emu-const>empty</emu-const>
            </td>
            <td>
              The event of the most recent leaving of its critical section, or <emu-const>empty</emu-const> if its critical section has never been entered.
            </td>
          </tr>
        </table>
      </emu-table>
      <p>There can be multiple <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-waiter-record"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-waiter-record">Waiter Records</a></emu-xref> in a WaiterList with the same <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-agents"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-agents">agent signifier</a></emu-xref>.</p>
      <p>The <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-agent-clusters"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-agent-clusters">agent cluster</a></emu-xref> has a store of WaiterList Records; the store is indexed by (<var>block</var>, <var>i</var>), where <var>block</var> is a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-data-blocks"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-data-blocks">Shared Data Block</a></emu-xref> and <var>i</var> a byte offset into the memory of <var>block</var>. WaiterList Records are <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent">agent</a></emu-xref>-independent: a lookup in the store of WaiterList Records by (<var>block</var>, <var>i</var>) will result in the same WaiterList Record in any <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent">agent</a></emu-xref> in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-agent-clusters"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-agent-clusters">agent cluster</a></emu-xref>.</p>
      <p>Each WaiterList Record has a <dfn variants="critical sections">critical section</dfn> that controls exclusive access to that WaiterList Record during evaluation. Only a single <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent">agent</a></emu-xref> may enter a WaiterList Record's critical section at one time. Entering and leaving a WaiterList Record's critical section is controlled by the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations">abstract operations</a></emu-xref> <emu-xref aoid="EnterCriticalSection"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-entercriticalsection">EnterCriticalSection</a></emu-xref> and <emu-xref aoid="LeaveCriticalSection"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-leavecriticalsection">LeaveCriticalSection</a></emu-xref>. Operations on a WaiterList Record—adding and removing waiting <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent">agents</a></emu-xref>, traversing the list of <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent">agents</a></emu-xref>, suspending and notifying <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent">agents</a></emu-xref> on the list, setting and retrieving the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model-fundamentals"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model-fundamentals">Synchronize event</a></emu-xref>—may only be performed by <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent">agents</a></emu-xref> that have entered the WaiterList Record's critical section.</p>
    df:["$","emu-clause",null,{"id":"sec-waiterlist-records","children":[["$","h1",null,{"id":"2542-waiterlist-records","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-bold x:mt-2 x:text-4xl","children":[[["$","span",null,{"className":"secnum","children":"25.4.2"}]," WaiterList Records"],["$","$L98",null,{"id":"2542-waiterlist-records"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"$13e"}}]]}]

      <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>This function is an optimization primitive. The intuition is that if the atomic step of an atomic primitive (<code>compareExchange</code>, <code>load</code>, <code>store</code>, <code>add</code>, <code>sub</code>, <code>and</code>, <code>or</code>, <code>xor</code>, or <code>exchange</code>) on a datum of size <var>n</var> bytes will be performed without the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#surrounding-agent"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#surrounding-agent">surrounding agent</a></emu-xref> acquiring a lock outside the <var>n</var> bytes comprising the datum, then <code>Atomics.isLockFree</code>(<var>n</var>) will return <emu-val>true</emu-val>. High-performance algorithms will use this function to determine whether to use locks or atomic operations in <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-waiterlist-records"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-waiterlist-records">critical sections</a></emu-xref>. If an atomic primitive is not lock-free then it is often more efficient for an algorithm to provide its own locking.</p>
        <p><code>Atomics.isLockFree</code>(4) always returns <emu-val>true</emu-val> as that can be supported on all known relevant hardware. Being able to assume this will generally simplify programs.</p>
        <p>Regardless of the value returned by this function, all atomic operations are guaranteed to be atomic. For example, they will never have a visible operation take place in the middle of the operation (e.g., “tearing”).</p>
      </div></emu-note>

157:T6b6,<p>This function provides a hint to the CPU that the program is spin looping while waiting on a value.</p>
      <p>It performs the following steps when called:</p>
      <emu-alg><ol><li>If the execution environment of the ECMAScript implementation supports signaling to the operating system or CPU that the current executing code is in a spin-wait loop, send that signal.</li><li class="exit">Return <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>.</li></ol></emu-alg>
      <emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>This method is designed for programs implementing spin-wait loops, such as spinlock fast paths inside of mutexes, to provide a hint to the CPU that it is spinning while waiting on a value. It has no observable behaviour other than timing.</p>
        <p>Implementations are expected to implement a pause or yield instruction if the best practices of the underlying architecture recommends such instructions in spin loops. For example, the <a href="https://www.intel.com/content/www/us/en/content-details/671488/intel-64-and-ia-32-architectures-optimization-reference-manual-volume-1.html">Intel Optimization Manual</a> recommends the <code>pause</code> instruction.</p>
        <p>Implementations are encouraged to have an internal upper bound on the maximum amount of time paused on the order of tens to hundreds of nanoseconds.</p>
      </div></emu-note>
      <emu-note data-num="2"><span class="note">Note 2</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>Due to the overhead of function calls, it is reasonable that an inlined call to this method in an optimizing compiler waits a different amount of time than a non-inlined call.</p>
      </div></emu-note>

15a:T1d62,<p>The abstract operation ParseJSON takes argument <var>text</var> (a String) and returns either a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">normal completion containing</a></emu-xref> a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">Record</a></emu-xref> with fields <var class="field">[[ParseNode]]</var> (a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-syntactic-grammar"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-syntactic-grammar">Parse Node</a></emu-xref>) and <var class="field">[[Value]]</var> (an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types">ECMAScript language value</a></emu-xref>), or a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">throw completion</a></emu-xref>.         <emu-alg><ol><li id="step-json-parse-validate" class="exit">If <emu-xref aoid="StringToCodePoints"><a <emu-val>SyntaxError</emu-val> exception.</li><li>Let <var>scriptString</var> be the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#string-concatenation"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#sec-object-initializer-static-semantics-early-errors">13.2.5.1</a></emu-xref> have special handling for the above invocation of <emu-xref aoid="ParseText"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-source-code#sec-parsetext">ParseText</a></emu-xref>.</li><li><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert"><a suppress-effects="user-code"><emu-xref aoid="Evaluation"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/syntax-directed-operations#sec-evaluation">Evaluation</a></emu-xref> of <var>script</var></emu-meta>.</li><li><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#note-step"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#note-step">NOTE</a></emu-xref>: The class="field">[[Value]]</var>: <var>result</var> }.</li></ol></emu-alg>
        <p>It is not permitted for a conforming implementation of <code>JSON.parse</code> to extend the JSON grammars. If an implementation wishes to support a modified or extended JSON interchange format it must do so by defining a different parse function.</p>
        <emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>Valid JSON text is a subset of the ECMAScript <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-PrimaryExpression">PrimaryExpression</a></emu-nt> syntax. Step <emu-xref href="#step-json-parse-validate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#step-json-parse-validate">1</a></emu-xref> verifies that <var>jsonString</var> conforms to that subset, and step <emu-xref href="#step-json-parse-assert-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#step-json-parse-assert-type">8</a></emu-xref> asserts that evaluation returns a value of an appropriate type.</p>
          <p>However, because <emu-xref href="#sec-runtime-semantics-propertydefinitionevaluation"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#sec-runtime-semantics-propertydefinitionevaluation">13.2.5.6</a></emu-xref> behaves differently during ParseJSON, the same source text can produce different results when evaluated as a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-PrimaryExpression">PrimaryExpression</a></emu-nt> rather than as JSON. Furthermore, the Early Error for duplicate <emu-val>"__proto__"</emu-val> properties in object literals, which likewise does not apply during ParseJSON, means that not all texts accepted by ParseJSON are valid as a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-PrimaryExpression">PrimaryExpression</a></emu-nt>, despite matching the grammar.</p>
        </div></emu-note>
        <emu-note data-num="2"><span class="note">Note 2</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>In the case where there are duplicate name Strings within an object, lexically preceding values for the same key shall be overwritten.</p>
        </div></emu-note>

              <td>The context <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-syntactic-grammar"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-syntactic-grammar">Parse Node</a></emu-xref>.</td>

      <p>The <emu-val>"length"</emu-val> property of this function is <emu-val>3</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>.</p>
      <emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>JSON structures are allowed to be nested to any depth, but they must be acyclic. If <var>value</var> is or contains a cyclic structure, then this function must throw a <emu-val>TypeError</emu-val> exception. This is an example of a value that cannot be stringified:</p>
        <pre><code class="hljs javascript">
          a = [];
          a[<span class="hljs-number">0</span>] = a;
          my_text = <span class="hljs-title class_">JSON</span>.<span class="hljs-title function_">stringify</span>(a); <span class="hljs-comment">// This must throw a TypeError.</span>
        </code></pre>
      </div></emu-note>
      <emu-note data-num="2"><span class="note">Note 2</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>Symbolic primitive values are rendered as follows:</p>
        <ul>
          <li>
            The <emu-val>null</emu-val> value is rendered in JSON text as the String value <emu-val>"null"</emu-val>.
          </li>
          <li>
            The <emu-val>undefined</emu-val> value is not rendered.
          </li>
          <li>
            The <emu-val>true</emu-val> value is rendered in JSON text as the String value <emu-val>"true"</emu-val>.
          </li>
          <li>
            The <emu-val>false</emu-val> value is rendered in JSON text as the String value <emu-val>"false"</emu-val>.
          </li>
        </ul>
      </div></emu-note>
      <emu-note data-num="3"><span class="note">Note 3</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>String values are wrapped in QUOTATION MARK (<code>"</code>) code units. The code units <code>"</code> and <code>\\</code> are escaped with <code>\\</code> prefixes. Control characters code units are replaced with escape sequences <code>\\u</code>HHHH, or with the shorter forms, <code>\\b</code> (BACKSPACE), <code>\\f</code> (FORM FEED), <code>\\n</code> (LINE FEED), <code>\\r</code> (CARRIAGE RETURN), <code>\\t</code> (CHARACTER TABULATION).</p>
      </div></emu-note>
      <emu-note data-num="4"><span class="note">Note 4</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite">Finite</a></emu-xref> numbers are stringified as if by calling <emu-xref aoid="ToString"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-tostring">ToString</a></emu-xref>(<var>number</var>). <emu-val>NaN</emu-val> and <emu-val>Infinity</emu-val> regardless of sign are represented as the String value <emu-val>"null"</emu-val>.</p>
      </div></emu-note>
      <emu-note data-num="5"><span class="note">Note 5</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>Values that do not have a JSON representation (such as <emu-val>undefined</emu-val> and functions) do not produce a String. Instead they produce the <emu-val>undefined</emu-val> value. In arrays these values are represented as the String value <emu-val>"null"</emu-val>. In objects an unrepresentable value causes the property to be excluded from stringification.</p>
      </div></emu-note>
      <emu-note data-num="6"><span class="note">Note 6</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>An object is rendered as U+007B (LEFT CURLY BRACKET) followed by zero or more properties, separated with a U+002C (COMMA), closed with a U+007D (RIGHT CURLY BRACKET). A property is a quoted String representing the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-name"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-name">property name</a></emu-xref>, a U+003A (COLON), and then the stringified property value. An array is rendered as an opening U+005B (LEFT SQUARE BRACKET) followed by zero or more values, separated with a U+002C (COMMA), closed with a U+005D (RIGHT SQUARE BRACKET).</p>
      </div></emu-note>

        <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>Spurious failures of the compare-exchange to update the length are prohibited. If the bounds checking for the new length passes and the implementation is not out of memory, a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model-fundamentals"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model-fundamentals">ReadModifyWriteSharedMemory</a></emu-xref> event (i.e. a successful compare-exchange) is always added into the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-candidate-executions"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-candidate-executions">candidate execution</a></emu-xref>.</p>
          <p>Parallel calls to SharedArrayBuffer.prototype.grow are totally ordered. For example, consider two racing calls: <code>sab.grow(10)</code> and <code>sab.grow(20)</code>. One of the two calls is guaranteed to win the race. The call to <code>sab.grow(10)</code> will never shrink <code>sab</code> even if <code>sab.grow(20)</code> happened first; in that case it will instead throw a RangeError.</p>
        </div></emu-note>

        <p>EnterCriticalSection has <dfn>contention</dfn> when an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent">agent</a></emu-xref> attempting to enter the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-waiterlist-records"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-waiterlist-records">critical section</a></emu-xref> must wait for another <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent">agent</a></emu-xref> to leave it. When there is no contention, FIFO order of EnterCriticalSection calls is observable. When there is contention, an implementation may choose an arbitrary order but may not cause an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent">agent</a></emu-xref> to wait indefinitely.</p>

        <emu-alg><ol><li class="exit">If <var>state</var>.<var class="field">[[Stack]]</var> contains <var>value</var>, throw a <emu-val>TypeError</emu-val> exception because the structure is cyclical.</li><li>Append <var>value</var> to <var>state</var>.<var class="field">[[Stack]]</var>.</li><li>Let <var>stepBack</var> be <var>state</var>.<var class="field">[[Indent]]</var>.</li><li>Set <var>state</var>.<var class="field">[[Indent]]</var> to the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#string-concatenation"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#string-concatenation">string-concatenation</a></emu-xref> of <var>state</var>.<var class="field">[[Indent]]</var> and <var>state</var>.<var class="field">[[Gap]]</var>.</li><li>If <var>state</var>.<var class="field">[[PropertyList]]</var> is not <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>, then<ol><li>Let <var>keys</var> be <var>state</var>.<var class="field">[[PropertyList]]</var>.</li></ol></li><li>Else,<ol><li>Let <var>keys</var> be ?         <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>The representation of arrays includes only the elements in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#interval"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#interval">interval</a></emu-xref> from <emu-val>+0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> (inclusive) to <code>array.length</code> (exclusive). Properties whose keys are not <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#array-index"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#array-index">array indices</a></emu-xref> are excluded from the stringification. An array is stringified as an opening LEFT SQUARE BRACKET, elements separated by COMMA, and a closing RIGHT SQUARE BRACKET.</p>
        </div></emu-note>
